4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.74635pt{\gamma}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-8.73286pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.93973pt{\gamma}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-7.92624pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
, and for
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
we let
\operatorname{lh}(s)
be the length of

s
, that is, the unique ordinal
\gamma<\kappa
such that
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.74635pt{\gamma}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-8.73286pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.93973pt{\gamma}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-7.92624pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
. The concatenation between two sequences
s,t
is denoted by

s{}^{\smallfrown}{}t
, and to simplify the notation we just write

s{}^{\smallfrown}{}i
and
i{}^{\smallfrown}{}s
if
t=\langle i\rangle
is a
sequence of length
1
.
If
\alpha\leq\operatorname{lh}(s)
, we denote by
s\restriction\alpha
the restriction of
s

to its first
\alpha
-many digits. We write
s\subseteq t
to say that
s
is an initial
segment of
t
, that is,
\operatorname{lh}(s)\leq\operatorname{lh}(t)
and
s=t\restriction\operatorname{lh}(s)
. The
sequences
s
and
t
are
comparable
if
s\subseteq t
or

t\subseteq s
, and
incomparable
otherwise.
A set
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is called
tree
if it is closed under
initial segments. For
\alpha<\kappa
we denote by
\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T)
the

\alpha
-th level of the tree
T
, namely,

\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T)=\{t\in T\mid\operatorname{lh}(t)=\alpha\}.

Given
s\in T
, we also define the localization of
T
at
s
as

T_{s}=\{t\in T\mid t\text{ is comparable with }s\}.

The bounded topology on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is the unique topology on such a space
with the following property:
a set
C\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is closed if and only it there is some tree

T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
such that
C=[T]
, where the
body

[T]
of the tree
T
is defined by

[T]=\{x\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}\mid\forall\alpha<\kappa\,(x\restriction\alpha\in T)\}.

The above tree
T
can always be required to be
pruned
, that is, such that for all

s\in T
and
\operatorname{lh}(s)\leq\alpha<\kappa
there is
s\subseteq t\in T
such that

\operatorname{lh}(t)=\alpha
, i.e.
\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T_{s})\neq\emptyset
for all
\alpha<\kappa
. Indeed, if
C
is closed, then the tree

T_{C}=\{x\restriction\alpha\mid x\in C\wedge\alpha<\kappa\}
is pruned
and such that
C=[T_{C}]
. Sometimes, one needs to consider a further closure property for trees. We say that the tree
T
is
{<}\kappa
-closed
if for all
sequences
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.74635pt{\gamma}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-8.73286pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.93973pt{\gamma}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-7.92624pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
with
\gamma<\kappa
limit, if

s\restriction\alpha\in T
for all
\alpha<\gamma
, then
s\in T
as well. A tree

T
is called
superclosed
if it is pruned and
{<}\kappa
-closed; this in
particular implies that if
s\in T
, then
\mathbf{N}_{s}\cap[T]\neq\emptyset
or,
equivalently,
[T_{s}]\neq\emptyset
. Not all closed subsets of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
are the body of a superclosed tree: consider e.g. the set

(2.3)

X_{0}=\{x\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}\mid|\{\alpha<\kappa\mid x(\alpha)=0\}|<\aleph_{0}\}.

This justifies the following terminology: a closed
C\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is called
superclosed
if
C=[T]
for some superclosed tree
T
.

Sikorski proved in
[
22
, Theorem (x)]
that every regular
\kappa
-additive space
of weight
\leq\kappa
is homeomorphic to a subspace of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
, and that
the latter is
\mathbb{G}
-metrizable.
We can sum up his results as follows, where we additionally
use Fact
2.8
to further add item
(d)
to the list of equivalent conditions.

Theorem 2.11

(
[
22
, Theorem (viii)-(x)]
)
.

For any space
X
of weight
\leq\kappa

and any totally ordered Abelian group
\mathbb{G}
with
\Deg(\mathbb{G})=\kappa

the following are equivalent:

(a)

X
is
\kappa
-additive;

(b)

X
is
\mathbb{G}
-metrizable;

(c)

X
is homeomorphic to a subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
;

(d)

X
is homeomorphic to a subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
.

Since conditions
(a)
,
(c)
, and
(d)
do not refer to
\mathbb{G}
at all,
this shows in particular that the choice of the actual group in the definition of the generalized metric is irrelevant.
We are now going to prove that analogous results hold also for
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces,
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces, and
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces (see Theorems
2.20
and
2.30
).

Proposition 2.12
.

Let
X
be a
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space. Then
X
is homeomorphic to a closed
C\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
.
If furthermore
X
is an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, then
C
can be taken to be superclosed.

Proof.

We prove the two statements simultaneously.
Let
(B_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}
be an enumeration of a clopen basis
\mathcal{B}
of
X
, possibly with repetitions.
Depending on whether
X
is an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space or just an
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, let
\sigma
be a winning strategy for player
\mathrm{II}
in
\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
or
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
.
By Remark
2.4
,
without loss of generality we can assume that the range of
\sigma
is contained in
\mathcal{B}
.
To simplify the notation, given an ordinal
\beta
, let
\mathrm{Succ}(\beta)
be the collection of all successor ordinals
\leq\beta
. Set also

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-25.03827pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-17.39531pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern We will construct a family of the form

and set for every

8

8

In our terminology, an indexed family
\{A_{i}\mid i\in I\}
of subsets of
X
is a partition of
X
if
\bigcup_{i\in I}A_{i}=X
and
A_{i}\cap A_{j}=\emptyset
for distinct
i,j\in I
.
In particular, some of the
A_{i}
’s might be empty and for
i\neq j
we have
A_{i}=A_{j}
if and only if both
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
are empty.

of

as follows.

.
Consider the tree

It is pruned by condition
(v’)
and the comment following it. Furthermore, if

-space (i.e.
\sigma
is a winning in the game

We now prove that the (super)closed set
C=[T]
is homeomorphic to
X
.
Since
\sigma
is a winning strategy in the strong (fair)
\kappa
-Choquet game, the
set
V(t)
is nonempty for every
t\in[T]
by condition
(ii)
and
equation (
2.6
) again, thus it contains exactly one point: let

f\colon[T]\to X
be the map that associates to every
t\in[T]
the unique element in
V(t)
.
We claim that
f
is a homeomorphism.

Claim 2.12.1
.

f
is bijective.

Proof.

To see that
f
is injective, let
t,t^{\prime}\in[T]
be distinct and
\alpha<\kappa
be such
that
t\restriction\alpha\neq t^{\prime}\restriction\alpha
. By condition
(v’)

we have

V(t\restriction\alpha)\cap V(t^{\prime}\restriction\alpha)=\emptyset
, and hence

f(t)\neq f(t^{\prime})
because
f(t)\in V(t)\subseteq V(t\restriction\alpha)
and

f(t^{\prime})\in V(t^{\prime})\subseteq V(t^{\prime}\restriction\alpha)
. To see that
f
is also surjective, fix
any
x\in X
.
By
(v’)
again (and the comment following it), for each
\alpha<\kappa

there is a unique
t_{\alpha}
of length
\alpha
with
x\in V(t_{\alpha})
, and
moreover
t_{\alpha}\subseteq t_{\beta}
for all
\alpha\leq\beta<\kappa
.
Let
t=\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}t_{\alpha}
, so that
x\in V(t)=\bigcap_{\alpha<\kappa}V(t_{\alpha})=\bigcap_{\alpha<\kappa}V(t\restriction\alpha)
: then
x
itself witnesses
t\in[T]
, and
f(t)=x
.
∎

Claim 2.12.2
.

f
is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

Observe that by definition of
f
, its surjectivity, and condition
(v’)
,

(2.7)

f(\mathbf{N}_{s}\cap[T])=V(s)=\hat{V}_{s}

for all
s\in T
with
\operatorname{lh}(s)\in\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)
. Since

\{\mathbf{N}_{s}\cap[T]\mid s\in T\cap\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-25.03827pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-25.03827pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-69.57814pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-17.39531pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-49.20947pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-16.58868pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-48.40285pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}\}
is a basis for the
relative topology of
[T]
, and since
\{\hat{V}_{s}\mid s\in T\cap\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-25.03827pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-25.03827pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-69.57814pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-17.39531pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-16.58868pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-48.40285pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}\}
is a basis for
X
by conditions
(i)
,
(iii)
, and
(v)
, then
f
and
f^{-1}
are continuous.
∎

It remains to construct the required family
\mathcal{F}
by recursion on
\gamma<\kappa
.
We assume that for every
t\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
with
\operatorname{lh}(t)=\gamma

and all
\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\gamma)
, the elements
x_{t\restriction\alpha}
,

U_{t\restriction\alpha}
,
V_{t\restriction\alpha}
, and
\hat{V}_{t\restriction\alpha}
have been defined so that conditions
(i)
–
(v)
are satisfied up
to level
\gamma
(when
\gamma>0
this is the inductive hypothesis, while if

\gamma=0
the assumption is obviously vacuous because
\mathrm{Succ}(0)
is empty): our
goal is to define
x_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}
,
U_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}
,

V_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}
, and
\hat{V}_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}
for all
t
as above
and
i<\kappa
in such a way that conditions
(i)
–
(v)
are preserved.

Recall the definition of the sets
V(t)
from equation (
2.4
).
If
V(t)=\emptyset
, then we set
U_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}=V_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}=\hat{V}_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}=\emptyset
for all
i<\kappa
and let
x_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}
be an arbitrary point in
X
.
Assume now that
V(t)\neq\emptyset
.
Notice that
V(t)
is clopen:
if
\gamma>0
this follows from
\kappa
-additivity of
X
and the fact that
\hat{V}_{t\restriction\alpha}
is clopen for every
\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\gamma)
by
(i)
, while if
\gamma=0
then
V(\emptyset)=X
by definition.
By condition
(ii)
, the sequence
\langle(U_{t\restriction\alpha},x_{t\restriction\alpha}),V_{t\restriction\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\gamma)\rangle
is a partial run in the corresponding Choquet-like game in which
\mathrm{II}
is following
\sigma
.
We let such run continue for one more round by letting
\mathrm{I}
play some
(U,x)
with

U
clopen and
x\in U\subseteq V(t)
, and
\mathrm{II}
reply with some

V\in\mathcal{B}
following the winning strategy
\sigma
, so that in particular

x\in V\subseteq U
. Let
\{V_{j}\mid j<\delta\}
be the collection of all those sets

V
that can be obtained in this way: even if there are possibly more than

\kappa
-many moves for
\mathrm{I}
as above, there are at most
\kappa
-many replies
of
\mathrm{II}
because
|\mathcal{B}|\leq\kappa
, hence
\delta\leq\kappa
. For each

j<\delta
we then choose one of player
\mathrm{I}
’s moves
(U_{j},x_{j})
yielding
V_{j}

as
\mathrm{II}
’s reply. In particular,
x_{j}\in V_{j}\subseteq U_{j}
. Let
(\hat{V}_{i})_{i<\nu}
(where
\nu\leq\kappa
) be an enumeration without repetitions of the nonempty
sets in

\left\{\left(V_{j}\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{\ell<j}V_{\ell}\right)\cap B_{\gamma}\,\,\middle|\,\,j<\delta\right\}\cup\left\{\left(V_{j}\setminus\bigcup\nolimits_{\ell<j}V_{\ell}\right)\setminus B_{\gamma}\,\,\middle|\,\,j<\delta\right\},

and for each
i<\nu
let
j(i)<\delta\leq\kappa
be such that
\hat{V}_{i}\subseteq V_{j(i)}
.
Notice that the
\hat{V}_{i}
’s are clopen by
\kappa
-additivity again. Finally, set

\displaystyle x_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}

\displaystyle=x_{j(i)}

\displaystyle U_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}

\displaystyle=U_{j(i)}

\displaystyle V_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}

\displaystyle=V_{j(i)}

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}

\displaystyle=\hat{V}_{i}

if
i<\nu
, and
U_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}=V_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}=\hat{V}_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}=\emptyset
with
x_{t{}^{\smallfrown}{}i}
an arbitrary point of
X
if
\nu\leq i<\kappa
.

It is not hard to see that conditions
(i)
–
(iv)
are preserved by construction.
As for condition
(v)
, by inductive hypothesis (or
V(\emptyset)=X
if
\gamma=0
) we get
(v’)
at level
\gamma
, that is,

\{V(t)\mid t\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.74635pt{\gamma}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-8.73286pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.93973pt{\gamma}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-7.92624pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}\}

is a partition of
X
. Thus the desired result straightforwardly follows from the fact
that the
V_{j}
’s cover
V(t)
because in our construction player
\mathrm{I}
can play any

x\in V(t)
in her last round (paired with a suitable clopen set
U
such that

x\in U\subseteq V(t)
, which exists because
V(t)
is clopen).
∎

We now consider the problem of simultaneously embedding two
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces
X^{\prime}\subseteq X
into
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
. Applying Proposition
2.12
to
X
we get a closed
C
and a homeomorphism
f\colon C\to X
. If
X^{\prime}
is a closed in
X
, it follows that also
C^{\prime}=f^{-1}(X^{\prime})
is closed in
C
and hence in
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
. However, when
X^{\prime}
is an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space we would like to have that
C^{\prime}
is superclosed.
To this aim we need to modify our construction.

Proposition 2.13
.

Let
X
be a
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space and
X^{\prime}\subseteq X
be a closed
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-subspace. Then there is a closed
C\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
and a homeomorphism
f\colon C\to X
such that
C^{\prime}=f^{-1}(X^{\prime})
is superclosed.

Proof.

The idea is to apply the argument from the previous proof but starting with a strategy
\sigma
that is winning for
\mathrm{II}
in
f\mathrm{G}_{\kappa}^{s}(X)
and, when “restricted” to
X^{\prime}
, it is also winning in
\mathrm{G}_{\kappa}^{s}(X^{\prime})
. Let
\mathcal{B}
be a basis for
X
of size
\leq\kappa
.

Claim 2.13.1
.

There is a winning strategy
\sigma
for player
\mathrm{II}
in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
with range in
\mathcal{B}
such that for any
\gamma\leq\kappa
and for any (possibly partial) run
\langle(U_{\alpha},x_{\alpha}),V_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\gamma\rangle
in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
where player
\mathrm{II}
followed
\sigma
, one has
\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
if and only if
V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
for every
\alpha<\gamma
.

Proof of the claim.

Let
\sigma^{\prime}
be an arbitrary winning strategy for
\mathrm{II}
in
\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X^{\prime})
, and let
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
be a winning strategy for
\mathrm{II}
in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
with range contained in

\mathcal{B}
. Define the strategy
\sigma
as follows. Suppose that at stage
\alpha<\kappa
player
\mathrm{I}
has played the sequence
\langle(U_{\beta},x_{\beta})\mid\beta\leq\alpha\rangle
in the game
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
.

(1)

As long as all points
x_{\beta}
belongs to
X^{\prime}
, player
\mathrm{II}
considers the auxiliary partial play
\langle(U_{\beta}\cap X^{\prime},x_{\beta})\mid\beta\leq\alpha\rangle
of
\mathrm{I}
in
\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X^{\prime})
and she uses
\tau^{\prime}
to get her next move
V^{\prime}_{\alpha}
in the game
\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X^{\prime})
. Since
V^{\prime}_{\alpha}
is open in
X^{\prime}
, there is
W
open in
X
such that
V^{\prime}_{\alpha}=W\cap X^{\prime}
: let
\mathrm{II}
play any
V_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{B}
such that
x_{\alpha}\in V_{\alpha}\subseteq W\cap\bigcap_{\beta\leq\alpha}U_{\beta}
as her next move in the game
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
(this is possible because
W\cap\bigcap_{\beta\leq\alpha}U_{\beta}
is open by
\kappa
-additivity).

(2)

If
\alpha
is smallest such that
x_{\alpha}\notin X^{\prime}
, from that point on player
\mathrm{II}
uses her strategy
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
pretending that
(U_{\alpha}\setminus X^{\prime},x_{\alpha})
was the first move of
\mathrm{I}
in a new run of
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
.

We claim that
\sigma
is as required, so
fix any
\gamma\leq\kappa
. Let
\langle(U_{\alpha},x_{\alpha}),V_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\gamma\rangle

be a partial run in which
\mathrm{II}
followed
\sigma
and assume that
V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
for every
\alpha<\gamma
. By
(2)
this implies
that
x_{\alpha}\in X^{\prime}
for all
\alpha<\gamma
. If
\gamma=\alpha+1
is a
successor ordinal, then
\bigcap_{\beta<\gamma}V_{\beta}\cap X^{\prime}=V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
by assumption. Assume instead that
\gamma
is limit. By
x_{\alpha}\in X^{\prime}
and
(1)
, for all
\alpha<\gamma

we have

(2.8)

U_{\alpha+1}\cap X^{\prime}\subseteq V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\subseteq V^{\prime}_{\alpha}\subseteq U_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime},

where
V^{\prime}_{\alpha}\subseteq X^{\prime}
is again
\mathrm{II}
’s reply to the
partial play
\langle(U_{\beta}\cap X^{\prime},x_{\beta})\mid\beta\leq\alpha\rangle
of

\mathrm{I}
in
\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X^{\prime})
according to
\sigma^{\prime}
. It follows that

\langle(U_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime},x_{\alpha}),V^{\prime}_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\gamma\rangle

is a (legal)
partial run in
\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X^{\prime})
where
\mathrm{II}
followed
\sigma^{\prime}
, and since the
latter is winning in such game we get
\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}=\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}V^{\prime}_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
(the first equality follows from (
2.8
) and the fact that
\gamma
is limit). This also implies that
\sigma
wins
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
in all runs where
V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
for all
\alpha<\kappa
; on the other hand, when this is not the case and
\alpha<\kappa
is smallest such that
V_{\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}=\emptyset
, then the tail of the run from level
\alpha
on is a (legal) run in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
in which
\mathrm{II}
followed
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, thus
\mathrm{II}
won as well. This shows that
\sigma
is winning for
\mathrm{II}
in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
and concludes the proof.
∎

Starting from
\sigma
as in Claim
2.13.1
, argue as
in the proof of Proposition
2.12
to
build a family
\mathcal{F}=\left\{\langle x_{s},U_{s},V_{s},\hat{V}_{s}\rangle\mid s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-25.03827pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-69.57814pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-25.03827pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-69.57814pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-17.39531pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-49.20947pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-16.58868pt{<\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-48.40285pt\mathchoice{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[33.12631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[20.2454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.46101pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}\right\}

and a homeomorphism

f\colon C\to X
, where
C=[T]
is the closed subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
defined by the tree
T=\{t\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}\mid V(t)\neq\emptyset\}
, and
f(t)
is the unique point in
V(t)
for all
t\in[T]
.
Consider now the tree defined by

T^{\prime}=\{t\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}\mid V(t)\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset\}.

Clearly
T^{\prime}\subseteq T
. Moreover, for every
t\in T^{\prime}
we have
\mathbf{N}_{t}\cap[T^{\prime}]\neq\emptyset
: indeed, if
t\in T^{\prime}
, then there is

x\in V(t)\cap X^{\prime}
, hence
f^{-1}(x)\supseteq t
and by construction
x
witnesses

f^{-1}(x)\restriction\alpha\in T^{\prime}
for all
\alpha<\kappa
, so
f^{-1}(x)\in\mathbf{N}_{t}\cap[T^{\prime}]
. In particular, this implies that
T^{\prime}
is pruned. We now prove that

T^{\prime}
is also superclosed. Let
t\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.32973pt{\gamma}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-12.91083pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.74635pt{\gamma}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-8.73286pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.93973pt{\gamma}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-7.92624pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
for
\gamma<\kappa

limit be such that
t\restriction\alpha\in T^{\prime}
for all
\alpha<\gamma
. Then

\hat{V}_{t\restriction\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
for all

\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\gamma)
, hence also
V_{t\restriction\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
by
\hat{V}_{t\restriction\alpha}\subseteq V_{t\restriction\alpha}
.
By the choice of
\sigma
, it follows that

\bigcap_{\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\gamma)}V_{t\restriction\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
,
hence
t\in T^{\prime}
since
V(t)=\bigcap_{\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\gamma)}V_{t\restriction\alpha}
when
t
has limit length.

Finally, we want to show that
f^{-1}(X^{\prime})=[T^{\prime}]
. Given
x\in X^{\prime}
, then
x
itself
witnesses
f^{-1}(x)\in[T^{\prime}]
. Conversely, if
t\in[T^{\prime}]
then
V_{t\restriction\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\supseteq V(t\restriction\alpha)\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
for all
\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)
, hence
by the choice of
\sigma
again we have that
\bigcap_{\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}V_{t\restriction\alpha}\cap X^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.
Since
\bigcap_{\alpha\in\mathrm{Succ}(\kappa)}V_{t\restriction\alpha}=V(t)=\{f(x)\}
,
it follows that
f(x)\in X^{\prime}
as desired.
∎

Proposition
2.13
allows us to considerably extend
[
13
, Proposition 1.3]
from superclosed subsets of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
to arbitrary closed
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-subspaces of a
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space.

Corollary 2.14
.

Let
X
be a
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space. Then every closed
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-subspace
Y
of
X
is a retract of it.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.13
,
without loss of generality we may assume that
X
is a closed subspace of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
and
Y\subseteq X
a superclosed set. By
[
13
, Proposition 1.3]
there is a retraction
r
from
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
onto
Y
. Then
r\restriction X
is a retraction of
X
onto
Y
.
∎

None of the conditions on
Y
can be dropped in the above result:
every retract of a Hausdorff space is necessarily closed in it, and
by
[
13
, Proposition 1.4]
the space
X_{0}
from equation (
2.3
) is a closed
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-subspace of the
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
which is not a retract of it. Notice also that there are even clopen (hence strong
\kappa
-Choquet) subspaces of
