Remark 2.17
.

Since
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is
\kappa
-additive and the latter is a hereditary
property, Theorems
2.15

and
2.16
can obviously be turned into a
characterization of
\kappa
-additivity inside the classes of
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces
and
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces, respectively.

Recall that an uncountable cardinal
\kappa
is (
strongly
)

inaccessible
if it is regular and strong limit, that is,
2^{\lambda}<\kappa
for
all
\lambda<\kappa
. An uncountable cardinal
\kappa
is
weakly
compact
if and only if it is inaccessible and has the tree property:
[T]\neq\emptyset

for every tree
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
satisfying
1\leq|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T)|<\kappa
for all
\alpha<\kappa
. A topological space
X
is

\kappa
-Lindelöf
if all its open coverings admit a subcovering of size
<\kappa
. (Thus
\omega
-Lindelöfness is ordinary compactness.)
It turns out that the space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
is
\kappa
-Lindelöf if and only if
\kappa
is weakly
compact
[
16
, Theorem 5.6]
, in which case
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
and

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}

is homeomorphic to
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}

. This implies that if

with

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
in
Theorem
2.16
as well. As for weakly compact cardinals

, the equivalence between
(a)
and

in

still holds replacing

with
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
by Fact
2.8
, but
the same does not apply to part
(c)
and
Theorem
2.16
because for such a
\kappa
all
(super)closed subsets of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
are
\kappa
-Lindelöf—see
Theorems
3.20
and
3.21
.

We now move to
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces. Our goal is to show that such spaces coincide
with the
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces, and thus that the definition is in
particular independent of the chosen
\mathbb{G}
. Along the way, we also generalize some
results independently obtained in
[
7
, Section 2.3]
and close some open
problems and conjectures contained therein, obtaining a fairly complete picture of the
relationships among all the proposed generalizations of Polish spaces.

In the subsequent results,
\mathbb{G}
is a totally ordered Abelian group with

\Deg(\mathbb{G})=\kappa
.
Examples of groups of this form are
\mathbb{Z}^{\kappa}
and
\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}
(and any other
(\mathbb{G}^{\prime})^{\kappa}
for an Abelian group
\mathbb{G}^{\prime}
) equipped with coordinate-wise operations and lexicographic orders,
the “
\kappa
-versions of the reals” proposed in
[
2
]
or in
[
7
]
, or any non-standard model of the reals of degree
\kappa
.

The next lemma was essentially proved
in
[
22
, Theorem (viii)]
and it corresponds to
(b)

\Rightarrow

(a)
in Theorem
2.11
.
We reprove it here for the reader’s convenience.

Lemma 2.18
.

Every
\mathbb{G}
-metric space
X
is
\kappa
-additive, hence also zero-dimensional.

Proof.

Let
\gamma<\kappa
and

(U_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\gamma}
be a sequence of nonempy open sets. If
\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}U_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
, consider an arbitrary

x\in\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}U_{\alpha}
.
The family
\{B_{d}(x,\varepsilon)\mid\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}\}
is a local basis of
x
, so for every
\alpha<\gamma
we may find
\varepsilon_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
such that

B_{d}(x,\varepsilon_{\alpha})\subseteq U_{\alpha}
. Since
\mathrm{Deg}(\mathbb{G})=\kappa>\gamma
, there is

\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
such that
\varepsilon\leq_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon_{\alpha}
for all

\alpha<\gamma
: thus
x\in B_{d}(x,\varepsilon)\subseteq\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}B_{d}(x,\varepsilon_{\alpha})\subseteq\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}U_{\alpha}
.
∎

Lemma 2.19
.

Every
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space
X
is strong fair
\kappa
-Choquet.

Proof.

Fix a compatible Cauchy-complete metric
d
on
X
and a strictly decreasing sequence
(r_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}
coinitial in
\mathbb{G}^{+}
.
Consider the strategy
\tau
of
\mathrm{II}
in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
in which he replies to
player
\mathrm{I}
’s move
(U_{\alpha},x_{\alpha})
by picking a ball

V_{\alpha}=B_{d}(x_{\alpha},\varepsilon_{\alpha})
with
\varepsilon_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{G}^{+}

small enough so that
\varepsilon_{\alpha}\leq_{\mathbb{G}}r_{\alpha}
and
\cl(V_{\alpha})\subseteq U_{\alpha}
. In particular, we will thus have
\cl(V_{\alpha+1})\subseteq V_{\alpha}
.
Suppose that
\langle(U_{\alpha},x_{\alpha}),V_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is a
run in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
in which
\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}V_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
for every limit
\gamma<\kappa
. Then
the choice of the
\varepsilon_{\alpha}
’s ensures that
(x_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}

is a Cauchy sequence, and thus it converges to some
x\in X
by Cauchy-completeness
of
d
. It follows that
x\in\bigcap_{\alpha<\kappa}\cl(V_{\alpha})=\bigcap_{\alpha<\kappa}V_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
, and thus
\tau
is a winning strategy for player
\mathrm{II}
.
∎

Theorem 2.20
.

For any space
X
the following are equivalent:

(a)

X
is
\mathbb{G}
-Polish;

(b)

X
is a
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space;

(c)

X
is homeomorphic to a
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
;

(d)

X
is homeomorphic to a closed subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
.

Proof.

The equivalence of
(b)
,
(c)
, and
(d)
is Theorem
2.15
, and
(d)
easily implies
(a)

(use e.g. the
\mathbb{G}
-metric described in equation (
2.1
)).
The remaining implication
(a)

\Rightarrow

(b)
follows from Lemma
2.18
and Lemma
2.19
.
∎

As usual, when
\kappa
is not weakly compact we can replace
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
with its homeomorphic copy
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
in conditions
(c)

and
(d)
above. When
\kappa
is instead weakly compact, by
Fact
2.8
we can still replace
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}

with
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
in condition
(c)
,
but the same does not apply to condition
(d)
because of

\kappa
-Lindelöfness—see Theorem
3.20
. In view of this
observation, the implication
(a)

\Rightarrow

(c)
in Theorem
2.20
is just a reformulation of
[
7
, Corollary 2.36]
, which is thus nicely complemented by the reverse implication
(c)

\Rightarrow

(a)
from our result.

Theorem
2.20
shows in particular that the notion of
\mathbb{G}
-Polish
space does not depend on the particular choice of the group
\mathbb{G}
.

Corollary 2.21
.

Let
\mathbb{G},\mathbb{G}^{\prime}
be two totally ordered (Abelian) groups, both of degree
\kappa
,
and
X
be a space.
Then
X
is
\mathbb{G}
-Polish if and only if it is
\mathbb{G}^{\prime}
-Polish.

For this reason, from now on will systematically avoid to specify which kind of
\mathbb{G}

we are considering and freely use the term “
\mathbb{G}
-Polish” as a shortcut for “
\mathbb{G}
-Polish with respect to a(ny) totally ordered (Abelian) group of degree
\kappa
”.

Remark 2.22
.

The only property of the metric
d
required in the proofs of Lemma
2.18
and Lemma
2.19

is that

(2.9)

\text{For all }x\in X,\text{ the family }\{B_{d}(x,\varepsilon)\mid\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}\}\mbox{ is a local basis of }x.

Hence, Theorem
2.11
and Theorem
2.20
(and
Corollary
2.21
) can be extended to metrics taking values in any other
kind of structure, as long as condition (
2.9
) is still
satisfied. (In particular, commutativity of
\mathbb{G}
is not really needed.)
This includes the case of
completely
S
-quasimetrizable spaces for a totally ordered semigroup
S
considered in
[
18
]
,
or spaces admitting a complete
\kappa
-ultrametric as defined
in
[
3
]
. In particular, the concepts of (complete) metric space
and (complete) ultrametric space lead to the same class of spaces in generalized
descriptive set theory. This is in strong contrast to what happens in the classical setting,
where Polish ultrametric spaces form a proper subclass of arbitrary Polish spaces
because admitting a compatible ultrametric implies zero-dimensionality.

Another easy corollary of Theorem
2.20
is that a
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}

subset of a
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space is necessarily
\mathbb{G}
-Polish as well (see the proof of Theorem
2.26
for details). We complement
this in Corollary
2.25
, using an extension result for continuous functions
(Proposition
2.24
). These results are the natural generalization of the
classical arguments in
[
10
, Theorems 3.8 and 3.11]
, and already
appeared in
[
7
, Theorems 2.34 and 2.35]
where, as customary in the subject, the fact that
\mathbb{G}
is Abelian is assumed and used.
However, we fully reprove both results for the sake of completeness and to confirm that
also in this case commutativity of
\mathbb{G}
is not required.

Lemma 2.23
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a totally ordered (non-necessarily Abelian) group
such that
\mathbb{G}^{+}
has no minimum.
Then for every
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
and every
n\in\omega
there is
\delta\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
with
10
10

10

As customary, we denote by
n\delta
the finite sum
\underbrace{\delta+_{\mathbb{G}}\dotsc+_{\mathbb{G}}\delta}_{n\text{ times}}
.

n\delta\leq_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon
.

Proof.

It is clearly enough to prove the result for
n=2
.
Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
be such that
0_{\mathbb{G}}<_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon^{\prime}<_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon
and set
\delta=\min\{\varepsilon^{\prime},-\varepsilon^{\prime}+_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon\}
. Since
\leq_{\mathbb{G}}
is translation-invariant on both sides we get

\delta+_{\mathbb{G}}\delta\leq_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon^{\prime}+_{\mathbb{G}}(-\varepsilon^{\prime}+_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon)=\varepsilon.\qed

Proposition 2.24
.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{G}
-metrizable space, and
(Y,d)
be a Cauchy-complete
\mathbb{G}
-metric space.
Let
A\subseteq X
be any set and
f\colon A\to Y
be continuous. Then there is a
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
set
B\subseteq X
and a continuous function
g\colon B\to Y
such that
A\subseteq B\subseteq\cl(A)
and
g
extends
f
, i.e.
g\restriction A=f
.

Proof.

Given any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
, let

O_{\varepsilon}
be the collection of those
x\in X
admitting an open neighborhood

U
such that
d(f(y),f(z))<_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon
for all
y,z\in U\cap A
. By
definition, each
O_{\varepsilon}
is open in
X
, and since
f\colon A\to Y
is
continuous then
A\subseteq O_{\varepsilon}
for all
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
(here
we are implicitly using Lemma
2.23
). Fix a strictly decreasing
sequence
(r_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}
coinitial in
\mathbb{G}^{+}
, and set

B=\cl(A)\cap\bigcap_{\alpha<\kappa}O_{r_{\alpha}},

so that
A\subseteq B\subseteq\cl(A)
and
B
is
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
by Lemma
2.9
.
Fix
x\in B
, and for every
\alpha<\kappa
fix an open neighborhood
U^{x}_{\alpha}
of

x
witnessing
x\in O_{r_{\alpha}}
.
Without loss of generality we
may assume that
U^{x}_{\beta}\subseteq U^{x}_{\alpha}
if
\alpha\leq\beta<\kappa
(if not, then
\tilde{U}^{x}_{\beta}=\bigcap_{\zeta\leq\beta}U^{x}_{\zeta}
is as desired by
\kappa
-additivity of
X
).
Since
x\in B\subseteq\cl(A)
, for each
\alpha<\kappa
we can pick some
y_{\alpha}\in U^{x}_{\alpha}\cap A
. The sequence

(f(y_{\alpha}))_{\alpha<\kappa}
is
d
-Cauchy by construction, thus it converges to some
y\in Y

by Cauchy-completeness of
d
: set
g(x)=y
. By uniqueness of limits, it is easy to check that the
map
g
is well-defined (i.e. the value
g(x)
is independent of the choice of the
U^{x}_{\alpha}
’s and

y_{\alpha}
’s), and that
g(x)=f(x)
for all
x\in A
. It remains to show that
g
is also
continuous at every
x\in B
.
Given any
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
, we want to find an open neighborhood
U
of
x
such that

g(U\cap B)\subseteq B_{d}(g(x),\varepsilon)
. Let
U^{x}_{\alpha}
and
y_{\alpha}
be as in
the definition of
g(x)
. Using Lemma
2.23
,
find
\delta\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
such that
3\delta\leq_{\mathbb{G}}\varepsilon
. Let
\alpha
be large enough so that

d(f(y_{\alpha}),g(x))<\delta
and
r_{\alpha}<\delta
, so that

f(U^{x}_{\alpha}\cap A)\subseteq B_{d}\left(g(x),2\delta\right)
. We claim that

U=U^{x}_{\alpha}
is as required. Indeed, if
z\in U\cap B
, then when defining
g(z)
we may
without loss of generality pick
U^{z}_{\alpha}
so that
U^{z}_{\alpha}\subseteq U^{x}_{\alpha}
: it then
follows that

g(z)\in\cl(f(U^{z}_{\alpha}\cap A))\subseteq\cl(f(U^{x}_{\alpha}\cap A))\subseteq\cl\left(B_{d}\left(g(x),2\delta\right)\right)\subseteq B_{d}(g(x),\varepsilon),

as required.
∎

Corollary 2.25
.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{G}
-metrizable space, and let
Y\subseteq X
be a completely
\mathbb{G}
-metrizable subspace of
X
. Then
Y
is a
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
subset of
X
.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
2.24
with
A=Y
and
f
the identity map from
Y
to itself. The resulting
g\colon B\to Y

coincides with the identity map on
B
on a dense subset of their common domain: since both functions are continuous, they coincide.
It follows that
B=g(B)\subseteq Y
, hence

B=Y
and
Y
is
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
.
∎

In
[
7
]
it is asked whether the reverse implication holds, i.e. whether

G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
subsets of
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces need to be
\mathbb{G}
-Polish as well
(see the discussion in the paragraph after
[
7
, Theorem 2.10]
): our
Theorem
2.20
already yields a positive answer, and thus it allows us to
characterize which subspaces of a
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space are still
\mathbb{G}
-Polish.

Theorem 2.26
.

Let
X
be
\mathbb{G}
-Polish and
Y\subseteq X
. Then
Y
is
\mathbb{G}
-Polish if and only if
Y
is
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
in
X
.

Proof.

One direction follows from Corollary
2.25
. For the other direction,
since
X
is homeomorphic to a closed subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
by
Theorem
2.20
, every
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
subspace
Y\subseteq X

is homeomorphic to a
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
. Using
again Theorem
2.20
, it follows that
Y
is
\mathbb{G}
-Polish as well.
∎

By Theorem
2.20
, Theorem
2.26

admits a natural counterpart characterizing
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-subspaces of
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces.

To complete the description of how our classes of spaces relate to each other, we just
need to characterize those spaces which are in all of them and thus have the richest
structure (this includes e.g. the generalized Cantor and Baire spaces). To this aim, we
need to introduce one last notion inspired by
[
3
, Definition 6.1]

and
[
7
]
.

Definition 2.27
.

A
\mathbb{G}
-metric
d
on a space
X
is called
spherically complete
if the
intersection of every
decreasing
(with respect to inclusion) sequence of open balls is nonempty. If in the
definition we consider only sequences of order type
\kappa
(respectively,
{<}\kappa
or
{\leq}\kappa
) we say that the metric is
spherically
\kappa
-complete
(respectively,
spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete
or

spherically
{\leq}\kappa
-complete
).

Remark 2.28
.

Let
(X,d)
be a
\mathbb{G}
-metric space.

(i)

If the space
X
has weight
\leq\kappa
, then the metric
d
is spherically complete if and
only if it is spherically
{\leq}\kappa
-complete. For the non trivial direction, fix
a decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{i})_{i<\kappa}

coinitial in
\mathbb{G}^{+}

and consider
an arbitrary decreasing chain of balls
B_{\alpha}=B_{d}(x_{\alpha},r_{\alpha})
for

\alpha<\lambda
with
\lambda
a regular cardinal greater than
\kappa
. If for all

i<\kappa
there is
\alpha_{i}<\lambda
such that
r_{\alpha_{i}}<\varepsilon_{i}
,
then by spherically
\kappa
-completeness we have

\bigcap_{i<\kappa}B_{\alpha_{i}}=\{x\}
for some
x
. It follows that

B_{\alpha}=\{x\}
for all
\alpha\geq\sup_{i<\kappa}\alpha_{i}
, hence

\bigcap_{\alpha<\lambda}B_{\alpha}=\{x\}\neq\emptyset
. The remaining case is
when there is
\delta\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
such that
r_{\alpha}\geq\delta
for all
\alpha<\lambda
.
If
\bigcap_{\alpha<\lambda}B_{\alpha}=\emptyset
, then we could recursively construct an
increasing sequence
(\alpha_{\beta})_{\beta<\lambda}
of ordinals
<\lambda
such that

x_{\alpha_{\beta}}\notin B_{\alpha_{\beta^{\prime}}}
for all
\beta<\beta^{\prime}<\lambda
. By the case
assumption, we thus have
d(x_{\alpha_{\beta}},x_{\alpha_{\beta^{\prime}}})\geq\delta
for all
distinct
\beta,\beta^{\prime}<\lambda
, contradicting the fact that
X
has weight

\leq\kappa<\lambda
.

(ii)

If
d
is spherically
\kappa
-complete, then it is also Cauchy-complete (independently of the weight of the space). Thus if
d
is spherically complete, then it is both spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete and Cauchy-complete.

(iii)

The converse does not hold:
there are examples of
\mathbb{G}
-metric spaces
(X,d)
of
weight
\kappa
such that
d
is both Cauchy-complete and spherically

{<}\kappa
-complete, yet it is not spherically
\kappa
-complete.
For example, consider the subspace
X=\{x_{\alpha}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}\mid\alpha<\kappa\}
of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
, where
x_{\alpha}(\alpha)=1
and
x_{\alpha}(\beta)=0
for all
\beta\neq\alpha
. Fix a decreasing sequence
(r_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}
coinitial in
\mathbb{G}^{+}
and some
s\in\mathbb{G}^{+}
.
The ultrametric on
X

defined by
d(x_{\alpha},x_{\beta})=s+\max\{r_{\alpha},r_{\beta}\}
for distinct
\alpha,\beta<\kappa
is discrete and hence trivially Cauchy-complete. Moreover, it is
<\kappa
-spherically complete. But the decreasing sequence
(B_{d}(x_{\alpha},s+r_{\alpha}))_{\alpha<\kappa}
has empty intersection.
Thus for a given

\mathbb{G}
-metric
d
being Cauchy-complete and spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete is
strictly weaker than being spherically
(
{\leq}\kappa
-)
complete.

Definition 2.29
.

A
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space is
spherically
(
{<}\kappa
-
)
complete
if it admits a compatible Cauchy-complete metric which is also spherically (
{<}\kappa
-)complete.

In
[
7
]
, spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces are also called
strongly
\kappa
-Polish spaces. Although in view of
Remark
2.28
(iii)
this seems to be the weakest
among the two possibilities considered in
Definition
2.29
, it will follow from
Theorem
2.30
that they are indeed equivalent: if a space of
weight
\leq\kappa
admits a compatible Cauchy-complete spherically

{<}\kappa
-complete
\mathbb{G}
-metric, then it also admits a (possibly different)
compatible Cauchy-complete
\mathbb{G}
-metric which is (fully) spherically complete. We
point out that the implication
(c)

\Rightarrow

(a)
already appeared in
[
7
, Theorem 2.45]
,
although with a different terminology.

Theorem 2.30
.

For any space
X
the following are equivalent:

(a)

X
is a
\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space;

(b)

X
is both an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space and
\mathbb{G}
-Polish;

(c)

X
is a spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space;

(d)

X
is a spherically complete
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space;

(e)

X
is homeomorphic to a superclosed subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
.

Proof.

Item
(b)

implies
(a)
because all
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces are
\kappa
-additive (Lemma
2.18
), while
(a)

implies
(e)
by Theorem
2.16
. Moreover, any
superclosed subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is trivially spherically complete
with respect to the
\mathbb{G}
-metric on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
defined in
equation (
2.1
), thus
(e)

implies
(d)
, and
(d)

obviously implies
(c)
. Finally, to prove that
(c)

implies
(b)
, recall that every
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space
X
is an
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space by
Theorem
2.20
.
Fix a compatible spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete
\mathbb{G}
-metric on
X

and a winning strategy
\tau
for
\mathrm{II}
in
f\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
, and observe that by Remark
2.4

we can assume that
\tau
requires
\mathrm{II}
to play only open
d
-balls
V_{\alpha}
because the latter
form a basis for the topology of
X
. Then
\tau
is also winning in

\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
because spherically
{<}\kappa
-completeness implies that

\bigcap_{\alpha<\gamma}V_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
for every limit
\gamma<\kappa
.
∎

Theorems
2.20
and
2.30
allow us to reformulate our Corollary
2.14
on retractions in terms of
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces. (Again, we have that none of the conditions on
Y
can be dropped, see the comment after Corollary
2.14
.)

Corollary 2.31
.

If
X
is
\mathbb{G}
-Polish, then all its closed subspaces
Y
which are also spherically complete
\mathbb{G}
-Polish (possibly with respect to a different
\mathbb{G}
-metric) are retracts of
X
.

Moreover, using the results obtained so far, one can easily observe that the classes of
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces and
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces are incomparable with respect to inclusion.
On the one hand, there are
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces which are not
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces:
in
[
7
, Theorem 2.41]
it is observed that Sikorski’s
\kappa
-
\mathbb{R}
is such an
example, but it is also enough to consider any closed subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}

which is not strong
\kappa
-Choquet, such as the space
X_{0}
defined in
equation (
2.3
).
Conversely, there are
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces which are not
\mathbb{G}
-Polish (to the best
of our knowledge, examples of this kind were not yet provided in the literature): just
take any non-
\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, such as
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}

equipped with the order topology induced by the lexicographical ordering.

In a different direction, Theorem
2.30
allows us to characterize
inside one given class those spaces which happen to also belong to a different one in a
very natural way. For example, among
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces we can distinguish those
that are also
\mathbb{G}
-Polish by checking
\kappa
-additivity. Conversely, working in
the class of
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces we can isolate those spaces
X
in which player

\mathrm{II}
wins the strong
\kappa
-Choquet game
\mathrm{G}^{s}_{\kappa}(X)
by checking
spherical completeness.

Figure
1
sums up the relationship among the various
classes of (regular Hausdorff) spaces of weight
\leq\kappa
considered so
far. At the end of Section
3
we will further enrich
this picture by distinguishing the class of
\kappa
-Lindelöf spaces—see
Theorems
3.20
and
3.21
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}
-metrizable
or,

equivalently,
\displaystyle\kappa
-additive

(Up to homeomorphisms: subsets of
\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.26273pt{\kappa}\kern 4.57185pt}}_{{\kern-12.85262pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.57185pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.26273pt{\kappa}\kern 4.57185pt}}_{{\kern-12.85262pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.57185pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.50577pt{\kappa}\kern 2.54086pt}}_{{\kern-8.49854pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.54086pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.7798pt{\kappa}\kern 1.8149pt}}_{{\kern-7.77257pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.8149pt}}}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}
-Polish

(Up to homeomorphism: closed subsets of
\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.26273pt{\kappa}\kern 4.57185pt}}_{{\kern-12.85262pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.57185pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.26273pt{\kappa}\kern 4.57185pt}}_{{\kern-12.85262pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.57185pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.50577pt{\kappa}\kern 2.54086pt}}_{{\kern-8.49854pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.54086pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.7798pt{\kappa}\kern 1.8149pt}}_{{\kern-7.77257pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.8149pt}}}
)

{<}\kappa
-)complete
\displaystyle\mathbb{G}
