(Up to homeomorphism: superclosed subsets of
\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.26273pt{\kappa}\kern 4.57185pt}}_{{\kern-12.85262pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.57185pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.26273pt{\kappa}\kern 4.57185pt}}_{{\kern-12.85262pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.57185pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.50577pt{\kappa}\kern 2.54086pt}}_{{\kern-8.49854pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.54086pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-3.7798pt{\kappa}\kern 1.8149pt}}_{{\kern-7.77257pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.57185pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.54086pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8149pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.8149pt}}}
)

SC
κ
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}
SC
κ

Figure 1.
Relationships among different Polish-like classes.

Despite the fact that the classes we are considering are all different from each other, we
now show that
one can still pass from one to the other by changing (and sometimes even refining) the
underlying topology, yet maintaining the same notion of
\kappa
-Borelness.

Proposition 2.32
.

Let
(X,\tau)
be an
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space (respectively,
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space). Then there is
\tau^{\prime}\supseteq\tau
such that
\mathsf{Bor}_{\kappa}(X,\tau^{\prime})=\mathsf{Bor}_{\kappa}(X,\tau)
and
(X,\tau^{\prime})
is a
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space (respectively,
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space).

Proof.

It is enough to let
\tau^{\prime}
be the topology generated by the
{<}\kappa
-sized
intersections of
\tau
-open sets. Arguing as
in
[
3
, Proposition 4.3 and Lemma 4.4]
, player
\mathrm{II}
still has a
winning strategy in the relevant Choquet-like game on
(X,\tau^{\prime})
. Moreover the
weight of
(X,\tau^{\prime})
is still
\leq\kappa
because we assumed

\kappa^{<\kappa}=\kappa
. Finally,
\kappa
-Borel sets do not change because by
definition
\tau\subseteq\tau^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Bor}_{\kappa}(X,\tau)
.
∎

This allows us to strengthen
[
3
, Theorem 3.3]
and extend it to
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces.

Corollary 2.33
.

If
X
is an
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, then there is a pruned tree
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
and a continuous bijection
f\colon[T]\to X
. Moreover, if
X
is an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space then
T
can be taken to be superclosed.

Proof.

Refine the topology
\tau
of
X
to a topology
\tau^{\prime}\supseteq\tau
as in
Proposition
2.32
. Then use
Theorem
2.12
to find a pruned (superclosed, if
X
was
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
) tree
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
and a
homeomorphism
f\colon[T]\to(X,\tau^{\prime})
. Since
f
remains a continuous bijection
when stepping back to
\tau
, we get that
T
and
f
are as required.
∎

By Proposition
2.32
(together with Theorem
2.20
), every
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, and thus every
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, can be
turned into a
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space sharing the same
\kappa
-Borel structure by
suitably refining its topology.
In contrast, it is not always possible to
refine
the topology
\tau
of an

f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space
X
to turn it into an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, even if we start
with a
\kappa
-additive (hence
\mathbb{G}
-Polish) one and we further allow to change its

\kappa
-Borel structure. Indeed, as shown in the next example, there are

\kappa
-additive strong fair
\kappa
-Choquet (i.e.
\mathbb{G}
-Polish) spaces
(X,\tau)
such that for
every topology
\tau^{\prime}\supseteq\tau
, the space
(X,\tau^{\prime})
is not an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space.

Example 2.34
.

Consider a closed (hence
\mathbb{G}
-Polish) subspace
C\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
which is not a continuous image of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
. Such a set exists by

: we can e.g. let

be the set of

\kappa
(coded as elements of

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern via the usual Gödel pairing function).
If one could find a refinement

of the bounded topology

-space (recall that any

of

by
[

, Theorem 3.5]
and thus so
would be

, contradicting the choice of

.

Nevertheless, if we drop the requirement that
\tau^{\prime}
refines the original topology

\tau
of
X
, then we can get a result along the lines above. This is due to the next
technical lemma, which will be further extended in Section
4
(see
Corollary
4.3
).

Lemma 2.35
.

Every closed set
C\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is
\kappa
-Borel isomorphic to a superclosed set
C^{\prime}\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
.

Proof.

If
C
has
{\leq}\kappa
-many points, then any bijection between
C
and

C^{\prime}=\{\alpha\conc 0^{(\kappa)}\mid\alpha<|C|\}
,
where
0^{(\kappa)}
is the constant sequence with length
\kappa
and value
0
,
is a
\kappa
-Borel isomorphism between
C
and the superclosed set

C^{\prime}
, hence we may assume without loss of generality that
|C|>\kappa
. Let
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
be a pruned tree
such that
C=[T]
. Let
L(T)
be the set of sequences
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}

of limit length such that
s\notin T
but
s\restriction\alpha\in T
for all
\alpha<\operatorname{lh}(s)
. (Clearly, the set
L(T)
is empty if and only if
C
is already superclosed).
Set
C^{\prime}=[T^{\prime}]
with

T^{\prime}=T\cup\{s\conc 0^{(\alpha)}\mid s\in L(T)\wedge\alpha<\kappa\},

where
0^{(\alpha)}
denotes the sequence of length
\alpha
constantly equal to
0
.
The tree
T^{\prime}
is clearly pruned and
{<}\kappa
-closed, hence
C^{\prime}
is
superclosed.
Notice also that
C^{\prime}\setminus C=\{s{}^{\smallfrown}{}0^{(\kappa)}\mid s\in L(T)\}

has size
\leq\kappa
.
Pick a set
A\subseteq C
of size
\kappa
and fix any bijection

g\colon A\to A\cup(C^{\prime}\setminus C)
.
Since both
C
and
C^{\prime}
are Hausdorff, it is easy to check that the map

f\colon C\to C^{\prime},\qquad x\mapsto\begin{cases}g(x)&\text{if }x\in A\\
x&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

is a
\kappa
-Borel isomorphism.
∎

Combining this lemma with Proposition
2.32
and
Theorem
2.15
we thus get

Proposition 2.36
.

Let
(X,\tau)
be an
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space. Then there is a topology
\tau^{\prime}
on

X
such that
\mathsf{Bor}_{\kappa}(X,\tau^{\prime})=\mathsf{Bor}_{\kappa}(X,\tau)
and
(X,\tau^{\prime})
is a

\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space (equivalently, a spherically complete

\mathbb{G}
-Polish space).

As a corollary, we finally obtain:

Theorem 2.37
.

Up to
\kappa
-Borel isomorphism, the following classes of spaces are the
same:

(1)

f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces;

(2)

\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces;

(3)

\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces;

(4)

\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces or, equivalently, spherically complete
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces.

Theorem
2.37
shows that, as we already claimed after
Definition
2.1
, we can consider any class of Polish-like spaces to
generalize
(St.Bor. 1)
: they all yield the same notion, and it is thus not necessary
to formally specify one of them. Furthermore, in Section
4
we will
prove that the class of
\kappa
-Borel spaces obtained in this way coincide with the
class of all standard
\kappa
-Borel spaces as defined in
Definition
2.1
, so we do not even need to introduce a different
terminology.

The sweeping results obtained so far allow us to improve some results from the
literature and solve
some open problems contained therein, so let us conclude this
section with a brief discussion on this matter.
In
[
7
, Theorem 2.51]
it is proved that, in our terminology, if
X
is a
spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space and
\kappa
is weakly
compact, then every
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-subspace
Y\subseteq X
is
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}

in
X
.
By Theorem
2.20
and Corollary
2.25
, we actually have
that every
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-subspace
Y
of a
\mathbb{G}
-metrizable space
X
is

G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
in
X
: hence the further hypotheses on
\kappa
and
X

required in
[
7
, Theorem 2.51]
are not necessary.
Furthermore, in
[
7
, Lemma 2.47]
the converse is shown to hold assuming that

X
is a
\mathbb{G}
-metric
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space (which through
\kappa
-additivity
implies that
X
is
\mathbb{G}
-Polish by Theorem
2.30
) and

Y
is spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete. Theorems
2.26

and
2.30
show that we can again weaken the hypotheses on

X
by dropping the requirement that
X
be a
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space:
if
X
is
\mathbb{G}
-Polish and
Y\subseteq X
is spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete
and
G^{\kappa}_{\delta}
, then
Y
is a
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space.
Finally, Theorem
2.30
shows that
[
7
, Theorem 2.53]

and
[
3
, Proposition 3.1]
deal with the same phenomenon: if
X

is a
\kappa
-perfect
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space, then there is a continuous injection
f
from the
generalized Cantor space into
X
, and if furthermore
X
is
\kappa
-additive, then
f

can be taken to be a homeomorphism on the image. The latter fact will be slightly improved in
Theorem
3.6
, where we show that in the
latter case the range of
f
can be taken to be superclosed.

, p. 25]

]

Here is a list of our improvements:

•

First of all, the ambient space
X
can be any
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space, and need not be spherically complete as assumed in
[
7
]
.

•

The new Arrows 8 and 9 hold because by
\mathbb{G}
-metrizability

is
\kappa
-additive, and hence a spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space.

•

Arrow 10 holds as well by Theorem
2.26
.

•

The implication Arrow 2 holds unconditionally (
\kappa
does not need to be weakly compact, as originally required in
[
7
]
).

•

The requirement that, in our terminology,
Y
be spherically
{<}\kappa
-complete cannot instead be
dropped in the implication Arrow 3: indeed, there are even closed subsets of

X=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}

, and hence they are not strong

3.
Characterizations of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}

The (classical) Cantor and Baire spaces play a central role in classical descriptive set
theory. It is remarkable that they admit a purely topological characterization
(see
[
10
, Theorems 7.4 and 7.7]
).

Theorem 3.1
.

(1)

(Brouwer) Up to homeomorphism, the Cantor space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-7.14726pt{\omega}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.8573pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-7.14726pt{\omega}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.8573pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.7397pt{\omega}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.5326pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.0397pt{\omega}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.8326pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
is the unique nonempty perfect compact metrizable zero-dimensional space.

(2)

(Alexandrov-Urysohn) Up to homeomorphism, the Baire space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-5.33972pt{\omega}\kern 3.05002pt}}_{{\kern-10.13261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.05002pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-4.46829pt{\omega}\kern 2.17859pt}}_{{\kern-9.26118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.17859pt}}}
is the unique nonempty Polish zero-dimensional space such that
all its compact subsets have empty interior.

Our next goal is to find analogous characterizations of the generalized Baire and Cantor
spaces. To this aim, we first have to generalize the above mentioned topological notions
to our setup.

First of all, we notice that a special feature of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
and

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
which is not shared by some of the other
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces is

\kappa
-additivity: since this condition already implies that the space be
zero-dimensional, the latter will always be absorbed by
\kappa
-additivity and will not
explicitly appear in our statements.
As for compactness, it is natural to replace it with the property of being

\kappa
-Lindelöf. Notice that this condition may play a role in the characterization
of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
only when

is weakly compact, as otherwise

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
is not
\kappa
-Lindelöf. However, this is not a true limitation,
because if

is not weakly compact, then the spaces
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
and

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
are homeomorphic, and thus the characterization of

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
takes care of both. In view of the Hurewicz
dichotomy
[
10
, Theorem 7.10]
, which
in
[
12
]
has been analyzed in detail in the context of
generalized descriptive set theory, we will also consider
K_{\kappa}
-sets
,
i.e. sets in a topological space which can be written as unions of
\kappa
-many

\kappa
-Lindelöf sets.

We now come to perfectness.
The notion of an isolated point may be transferred to the generalized context in (at least) two natural ways:

•

keeping the original definition: a point
x
is
isolated
in
X
if there is an open set
U\subseteq X
such that
U=\{x\}
;

•

allowing short intersections of open sets
(see e.g.
[
3
, Section 3]
): a point
x
is
\kappa
-isolated
in
X
if there are
{<}\kappa
-many open sets whose intersection is
\{x\}
.

A topological space is then called (
\kappa
-
)
perfect
if it has no (
\kappa
-)isolated points.

If we restrict the attention to
\kappa
-additive spaces, as we do in this section, the two notions coincide.
However, the notion of
\kappa
-perfectness is in a sense preferable when the space

X
is not
\kappa
-additive because it implies that
X
has weight at least
\kappa

and that all its nonempty open sets have size
\geq\kappa
(use the regularity of

\kappa
and the fact that all our spaces are Hausdorff). If we further require
X
to
be strong
\kappa
-Choquet, we get the following strengthening of the last property.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
X
be an
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space. If
X
is
\kappa
-perfect, then every open set
U\subseteq X
has size
2^{\kappa}
.

Proof.

If
X
is
\kappa
-perfect, then so is every open
U\subseteq X
. Since
U
is
strong
\kappa
-Choquet as well, there is a continuous injection from

\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
into
U
by
[
3
, Proposition 3.1]
, hence

|U|=2^{\kappa}
.
∎

In the statement of Lemma
3.2
one could further replace the open set

U
with a
{<}\kappa
-sized intersection of open sets.
The lemma is instead not true for arbitrary
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces, even when requiring

\kappa
-additivity (and thus it does not work for arbitrary
\mathbb{G}
-Polish spaces either).
For a counterexample,
consider the closed subspace
X_{0}
of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
defined in
equation (
2.3
):
by Theorem
2.20
,
X_{0}
is a
\kappa
-additive
f\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space (equivalently, a
\mathbb{G}
-Polish space), it is clearly
\kappa
-perfect, yet it has
size
\kappa
.

In the next lemma we crucially use the fact that we assumed

\kappa^{<\kappa}=\kappa
.

Lemma 3.3
.

If
Y
is a
T_{0}
-space of size
>\kappa
, then
Y
has weight
\geq\kappa
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be any basis of
Y
. Then the map sending each point of
Y
into the set of its basic open neighborhoods is an injection into

\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{B})
. Thus if there is such a
\mathcal{B}
of size
\nu<\kappa
then
|Y|\leq 2^{\nu}\leq\kappa^{<\kappa}=\kappa
.
∎

A tree
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
is
splitting
if for every
s\in T
there are incomparable
t,t^{\prime}\in T
extending
s
(without loss of generality we can further require that
\operatorname{lh}(t)=\operatorname{lh}(t^{\prime})
).
We now show that the splitting condition captures the topological notion of perfectness
for
\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces. (Notice that the equivalence between
items
(a)

and
(e)
in
Lemma
3.4
may be seen as the analogue of
Theorem
2.16
for (
\kappa
-)perfect

\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-spaces.)

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
X
be a nonempty
\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space. The following are equivalent:

(a)

X
is (
\kappa
-)perfect;

(b)

every nonempty open subset of
X
has size
>\kappa
;

(c)

every nonempty open subspace of
X
has weight
\kappa
;

(d)

every superclosed
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
such that
X
is homeomorphic to
[T]
is splitting;

(e)

there is a splitting superclosed
11
11

11

This is a bit redundant: if
T
is splitting and
{<}\kappa
-closed, then it is automatically pruned.

tree
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
with
[T]
homeomorphic to
X
.

Proof.

The implication

(a)

\Rightarrow

(b)
follows from
Lemma
3.2
, while the implication

(b)

\Rightarrow

(c)
follows from
Lemma
3.3
and the fact that
X
has weight (at most)
\kappa
.
In order to prove
(c)

\Rightarrow

(d)
, notice that if

s\in T
then
\mathbf{N}_{s}\cap[T]\neq\emptyset
because
T
is superclosed. Thus
s

must have two incomparable extensions, since otherwise
\mathbf{N}_{s}\cap[T]
would be
a nonempty open set of weight (and size)
1
. The
implication
(d)

\Rightarrow

(e)

follows from Theorem
2.16
, which ensures the
existence of a superclosed
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
with
[T]

homeomorphic to
X
: such a
T
is then necessarily splitting by
condition
(d)
.
Finally, for the implication
(e)

\Rightarrow

(a)

notice that if
T
is splitting and superclosed, then for every two incomparable
extensions
t,t^{\prime}\in T
of a given
s\in T
we have

\mathbf{N}_{t}\cap[T]\neq\emptyset
and
\mathbf{N}_{t^{\prime}}\cap[T]\neq\emptyset
but

\mathbf{N}_{t}\cap\mathbf{N}_{t^{\prime}}=\emptyset
, hence
|\mathbf{N}_{s}\cap[T]|>1
for all

s\in T
.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

Notice that if
\kappa
is inaccessible, then the splitting condition on the superclosed
tree
T
in items
(d)
and
(e)

above can be strengthened to

(3.1)

\forall s\in T\,\forall\nu<\kappa\,\exists\alpha<\kappa\,(\alpha>\operatorname{lh}(s)\wedge|\mathrm{Lev}_{\alpha}(T_{s})|\geq\nu).

Notice also that if
\alpha<\kappa
witnesses (
3.1
) for given
s\in T
and
\nu<\kappa
, then every
\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\kappa
witnesses the same fact because
T
is pruned.

Lemma
3.4
allows us to prove the following strengthening of
[
3
, Proposition 3.1]
and
[
7
, Theorem 2.53]
, answering in particular
[
3
, Question 3.2]
for the case of
\kappa
-additive spaces.

Theorem 3.6
.

Let
X
be a nonempty
\kappa
-additive
\mathrm{SC}_{\kappa}
-space. If
X
is (
\kappa
-)perfect, then there is a superclosed
C\subseteq X
which is homeomorphic to
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.92043pt{\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.13142pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.57767pt{\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.01408pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.87767pt{\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.31409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.4
we may assume that
X=[T]
with
T\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-11.42715pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-28.74477pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.67308pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-20.04282pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-6.86646pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-19.23619pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
superclosed and splitting.
Recursively define a map
\varphi\colon\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-7.2999pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-19.66963pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.5999pt{<\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-18.96964pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}\to T
by setting

\varphi(\emptyset)=\emptyset
and then letting
\varphi(t\conc 0)
and

\varphi(t\conc 1)
be incomparable extensions in
T
of the sequence of

\varphi(t)
. At limit levels we set
\varphi(t)=\bigcup_{\alpha<\operatorname{lh}(t)}\varphi(t\restriction\alpha)
, which is still an element of
T
because the latter is

{<}\kappa
-closed.

By construction,
\varphi
is a tree-embedding from
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-7.2999pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-19.66963pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.5999pt{<\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-18.96964pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
into
T
,
i.e.
\varphi
is monotone and preserves incomparability. Moreover,

\operatorname{lh}(\varphi(t))\geq\operatorname{lh}(t)
for every
t\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-7.2999pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-19.66963pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.5999pt{<\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-18.96964pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
.
Let

generated by

It is easy to see that

is pruned. We now want to check that it is also

for some

.
(We present a detailed argument because the claim uses in an essential way that

is finitely splitting, and would instead fail if e.g.

is replaced by

.)
Set
A=\{t\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-10.73154pt{<\kappa}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-28.04915pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-7.2999pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-19.66963pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.5999pt{<\kappa}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-18.96964pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}\mid\varphi(t)\subseteq s\}
.
Since
\varphi
preserves incomparability, all sequences in
A
are comparable and
thus the sequence
